To ensure that Leda cannot change her mind about publicity.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. To make Leda responsible for proving the resident’s identity.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. To give her a lasting record of the agreed boundaries beyond the group&#x27;s assurances of goodwill.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. To authorize the group to decide what her father should discuss.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"4\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"6\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v01c07","title":"Before the Visit","volume":"卷一｜假婚约与失踪证人","anchor":"chapter-0007","paragraphs":["At two forty, Leda stood outside Kingfisher House with a bowl of honey biscuits. She had changed her dress twice, then arrived in a plain jacket because her father had always preferred practical clothes. Mara carried the biscuits while Leda tried to fasten a button with trembling fingers.","\"I used to think adulthood meant never having to be nervous,\" Leda said.","\"A distasteful rumour,\" Mara replied. \"We should expose its author.\"","The advocate, a quiet woman called Amina Holt, arrived without a photographer. Her introductory letter gave her access to consenting residents and allowed her to seek an independent review of visiting restrictions. It did not permit her to force a conversation with someone who wished to be left alone.","Mara appreciated the limitation even while she feared what Dacre might do with it.","Inside, the reception smelled deliciously of baking bread. A fragrant candle burned beside a complimentary brochure. The perfume was so strong that Mara could hardly distinguish it from the soup she had smelled the day before. The room seemed to cater to anxious relatives by offering every sensory sign of home except an open door.","Dacre emerged wearing a violet tie.","\"You are early,\" he said.","Amina checked her watch. \"Four minutes. We can wait.\"","\"That won't be necessary. There has been a modification to the arrangements.\"","Noor's hand tightened on her bag. \"What modification?\"","\"Mr. Reed left this morning.\"","The words seemed to halt the entire building. Leda looked at the bowl in Mara's hands, as though she had suddenly forgotten its function.","\"You agreed to a visit,\" Amina said.","\"Before the treating team decided that a transfer was preferable.\"","\"Where?\"","\"I cannot disclose medical information to a third party without authorization.\"","\"Then ask him whether he authorizes it.\"","Dacre spread his hands. \"He is no longer here.\"","The reply was correct in form and empty in substance. Mara had heard similar language from creditors: an obligation arranged so that every route to fulfilling it depended on another route already being closed.","Leda put the biscuits on the desk. \"Did he know I was coming?\"","\"I was not present for every discussion.\"","\"Did anyone tell him?\"","\"I can't answer that.\"","She took a long breath. \"Then please stop speaking as if you know what he wanted.\"","Dacre's polished expression failed for a moment. Mara felt a fierce, tender pride that had no place to go.","Amina asked him to preserve the departure log, the authorization record, and the staff rota. Dacre said he would consult the institution's legal department. The advocate requested written confirmation and remained at the desk until he gave it.","Nothing in that document proved a crime. It did establish that he had been asked to preserve particular records on a particular day. June, who waited outside, would later say that paper rarely felt heroic while people were making it. Only after it survived did anyone notice its courage.","As they turned to leave, the young receptionist dropped a pen. Mara bent at the same time. The woman's hand closed over hers for one second.","\"Don't eat the green ones,\" she whispered.","Then she stood up, face bare of expression.","Outside, Leda opened the biscuit bowl. Mara had assumed the girl made all the biscuits herself. In fact, a theatre colleague had added a few decorated ones, including two green leaves. Leda lifted one. Beneath it lay a folded scrap.","Call after nine. No names. Ask for the person who misplaced the lamp.","There was a number below.","\"A breakthrough?\" Cassian asked.","\"An invitation,\" Mara said. \"Possibly a trap.\"","Leda wiped her eyes with the heel of her hand. \"I can be useful.\"","\"You don't have to earn help,\" Noor told her.","\"I know. I still want to be useful.\"","They agreed that she would return to the theatre with June. Mara would not have liked being put aside for her own protection; she tried not to offer Leda the same burden in a kinder voice. The choice remained hers. She chose company and work until the phone call.","Cassian and Mara walked north along a narrow street. A large group of schoolchildren crossed before them, shepherded by a civil servant from the public library. One boy wore ear defenders and concentrated on a painted line beneath his shoes. The adult gave him space, then waited while he adjusted to the noise.","Mara watched the small accommodation with an ache she could not quite name. Kindness often looked less impressive than power. It also appeared to require more attention.","\"You blame yourself,\" Cassian said.","\"We gave them a reason to move him.\"","\"We may have. They may have planned it beforehand. We don't yet know.\"","\"Does that make a difference to him?\"","\"It makes a difference to what we do next.\"","She stopped beside a tree growing through a cracked pavement square. \"You're very good at putting an argument between yourself and pain.\"","The remark landed harder than she intended. His gaze moved away.","\"I practise,\" he said.","She wished she could take it back without making another performance of regret. \"That was unfair.\"","\"Partly.\"","\"I can offer a complete apology.\"","\"I'd prefer the accurate one.\"","She looked at him. Rain had darkened the shoulders of his coat. He seemed tired in a mortal, ordinary way that the portraits and headlines had failed to portray.","\"I was angry,\" she said. \"And you were close enough to hurt.\"","\"Thank you for saying it.\"","He did not immediately offer forgiveness as a gift she must admire. They continued walking. After a while, he asked whether she was hungry.","At a stall under a railway bridge, they bought rice wrapped in a banana leaf. Cassian had never eaten there. Mara showed him where to put his feet so that passing bicycles would not splash his trousers. He obeyed with an earnestness that made her want to laugh and, more dangerously, touch him.","\"Do you always follow instructions so politely?\"","\"Only the sensible ones.\"","\"That must restrict your social life.\"","\"You have no idea.\"","He noticed the loose bandage on her palm and offered the spare one Noor had given him. He did not reach across the food to replace it himself. Mara opened the packet and discovered that her fingers had become awkward with cold.","\"Help?\" he asked.","She nodded. He held the end while she wrapped it, a small cooperation requiring neither rescue nor surrender. When she finished, he brushed a grain of rice from his sleeve with an entirely inappropriate air of triumph.","For a few minutes, they were two people eating lunch, rather than an engagement and an investigation struggling to occupy the same space. Mara wanted to preserve those minutes without converting them into a promise.","Then Cassian's phone sounded.","Ivo had recovered another fragment from the magnetic strip. It did not contain a voice. It held a timestamp and a portion of a delivery reference. The last characters corresponded to the Old Exchange warehouse.","They went there before the afternoon light faded.","The warehouse stood on a slope above a disused tram line. Its windows were boarded, but the old stage door remained intact. Mara recognised a relatively recent lock and an older, more expressive set of scratches around it.","\"Someone tried to enter badly,\" she said.","\"Can you do better?\"","\"Yes. But not while you stand there looking like a man about to carry out a search.\"","He leaned against the wall and took out his phone. \"Sight-seeing.\"","\"Too upright. Think idle wealth.\"","He lowered one shoulder. She gave him an approving nod.","The lock opened. Inside lay an abandoned feast of theatrical scraps: a broken throne, a silk wedding canopy, a rug painted to resemble marble. A drum waited beneath a sheet. Two artificial swans faced an empty champagne bottle, as though the wedding guests had left them in charge.","Mara switched on a lamp. Its yellow light illuminated a path through the dust. Most footprints were old. One set led towards a cupboard near the stage door.","There, beneath a loose shelf, they found a packet addressed to Tomas Reed.","It contained no medical record. There was a child's drawing of a chessboard, a photograph of Leda at twelve, and an unopened letter from a solicitor offering independent advocacy. The letter had been sent to Kingfisher, then redirected here.","\"Why store his post in a warehouse?\" Mara asked.","Cassian photographed the packet before moving it. \"It may have been collected during the transfer. Or someone wanted it away from the house.\"","A small sound came from outside.","Not a footstep. A key entering the lock.","Mara extinguished the lamp. Cassian moved beside her, leaving the narrow way to the rear exit open. In the dark, his hand found hers only after she reached for it.","The stage door began to swing inward."],"questions":[{"type":"scope","stem":"What does Cassian’s response to Mara’s self-blame class=\"meta\">先完成选择，再查看解析。练习内容不计入词汇正文覆盖。</p><p class=\"meta\" data-score=\"6\"></p><button type=\"button\" data-reset=\"6\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-7-1\" data-question=\"6,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. scope</p><p><strong>What does Cassian’s response to Mara’s self-blame suggest?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. The timing of the transfer proves that Dacre acted before their visit.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. Its cause is uncertain, and distinguishing possible explanations matters for their next action.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. Mara must stop asking whether their visit affected the resident.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The lack of certainty makes the transfer irrelevant to their investigation.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"5\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"7\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v01c08","title":"The Unfinished Letter","volume":"卷一｜假婚约与失踪证人","anchor":"chapter-0008","paragraphs":["The man who entered the warehouse carried a mop. He wore a brown coat with one sleeve patched in blue, and his first words were directed at the darkness.","\"If you're the landlord, the leak is still there.\"","Mara did not move. Cassian's hand remained steady around hers.","The man switched on his torch. Its beam crossed an artificial swan, climbed the wedding canopy, and stopped at two strangers standing beside an open cupboard.","\"Oh,\" he said. \"Burglars. That's an improvement. You might actually take the broken furniture.\"","Cassian released Mara's hand when she loosened her fingers. He introduced himself and asked whether the man worked there.","\"I used to. Then they sold the building and employed me to keep an eye out for squatters. Nobody told me whether I should count myself.\"","His name was Peter Moss. The warehouse's exterior had become unsafe after a winter storm, so the new administrator had closed the front rooms. Peter still used a little office at the back. He had been due to retire in eighteen months, but the ownership change had made that future unfamiliar.","\"You shouldn't be involved in this,\" Cassian said.","\"I am involved in the building. What is this?\"","Mara showed him Reed's name on the packet. Peter's expression changed.","\"He came here yesterday.\"","Cassian put away his phone. \"Alone?\"","\"With a man who stayed in the car. Tomas wanted his daughter's picture. He asked me to post a letter. Then he tore it up.\"","\"Why?\" Mara asked.","\"Said he'd forgotten how to begin.\"","Peter turned towards the back office. Mara and Cassian followed past a vertical stack of scenery flats and a patched curtain whose faded rainbow suggested better evenings. In a wastepaper basket, beneath a tea bag, lay several torn scraps.","Mara knelt to examine them. She did not assemble the letter at once. First she photographed the basket as they found it. The ordinary care seemed absurdly small beside the possibility that Reed was missing. Yet a hasty reconstruction could destroy the very relations they needed to comprehend.","Peter watched her work. \"You're the woman from the photograph.\"","\"Unfortunately.\"","\"It made you look like someone who'd steal the carpet.\"","\"I prefer portable things.\"","He laughed, then grew serious. \"Tomas said a woman would come in search of something that belonged to her. He didn't give a name.\"","Cassian and Mara looked at one another.","\"When?\" Cassian asked.","\"Yesterday afternoon. Before the photographs appeared.\"","The timing weakened one convenient explanation. Reed could not simply have repeated a headline he had already seen. It did not, however, establish that he had known Mara would come; the sentence might have referred to Leda or to another woman entirely.","Mara placed the pieces on a dark coat Peter spread across the desk. The repaired line began: If she has the flower, don't give them the record.","The next part was lost to a tea stain.","Beneath it, a second line survived: They will tell you I left of my own—","The paper ended there.","\"Free will,\" Mara murmured.","\"Possibly,\" Cassian said. \"Don't add it yet.\"","She looked up sharply, then saw that he was cautioning himself as much as her. She set the fragment down without finishing it.","Peter found an empty envelope in the same basket. Its stamp had not been cancelled, and Reed's name was written across the flap. Mara kept it separate from the scraps. Similar paper could support their belief that the pieces belonged together, but she would not let a pleasing fit become a substitute for a complete account. She had repaired too many objects that somebody else had confidently put together the wrong way.","Peter offered tea. They accepted because he seemed to need an everyday task. His hands shook as he put three mugs beside the kettle.","\"He looked well,\" he said. \"Better than when he came in the spring. That was why I didn't ask more.\"","\"Looking well doesn't prove that someone is safe,\" Mara said. \"It doesn't prove danger either. You couldn't have known.\"","She heard Noor in her own voice and was unexpectedly grateful.","Peter found a security camera mounted above the loading door. It was a cheap model with no subscription service; the recording remained on a card. Ivo, reached by phone, explained how to remove it without erasing the files. Peter gave permission for an independent copy and kept the original.","On the tiny screen, a car appeared at eleven sixteen. Reed climbed out carrying a folder. The driver remained behind dark glass. Seventeen minutes later, Reed returned without the folder.","\"Was that this morning?\" Cassian asked.","Peter checked the calendar on the wall. \"Yesterday. I told you.\"","The car's passenger window lowered as it left. For one second, a face appeared.","Mara recognised Dacre's receptionist.","\"She was the driver,\" she said.","\"Or the passenger,\" Cassian replied. \"The angle isn't clear.\"","The correction mattered. It left open a role she had been ready to close. A woman trying to help might also be part of the harm, but appearance alone could not decide which.","At nine, Mara called the number hidden beneath the green biscuit. A voice asked who she wanted.","\"The person who misplaced the lamp.\"","\"Wrong number.\"","The connection ended. Mara stared at the phone.","Then a message arrived: Laundry behind the old tram square. Ten minutes. Come on foot.","Cassian read it beside her. \"We can bring Noor.\"","\"She asked us to come on foot, not alone.\"","\"I agree.\"","Mara looked at him suspiciously.","\"Sometimes,\" he said, \"you have an excellent point.\"","The laundry remained open late for restaurant workers. Steam clouded its front window. A woman sat beneath a flickering light, folding white towels with an attention that bordered on obsession.","She did not look up when they entered.","\"You should have stayed away from the house,\" she said.","Mara set her phone on the counter without recording. \"You should tell us your name.\"","\"Nessa. That is all.\"","\"All right, Nessa.\"","The woman's eyes rose. \"You aren't going to ask for the rest?\"","\"You can decide whether to give it.\"","For several seconds, the machines supplied all the noise. Nessa folded a towel into a small square, then unfolded it.","\"I thought it was a good job. A reputable place. They said the restrictions were precautionary. People with memory injuries get confused. I didn't understand how often I was expected to confuse them myself.\"","\"What did you do?\" Noor asked.","\"Told them their families had cancelled. Told families the patients didn't want visits. Sometimes both on the same day.\"","Her confession did not arrive like a speech. It came in ordinary sentences that she seemed unable to stop arranging into something less inhumane.","Noor sat on a plastic stool. \"Have you kept anything that can help establish what happened?\"","\"Messages. Rosters. Some old shift instructions. Not all.\"","\"May we see them?\"","Nessa nodded. Then her attention moved to Cassian.","\"They said you were responsible for the fire. That if people remembered what happened, you would go to prison.\"","He grew still. \"Who said that?\"","\"The director. Once. Not Mr. Dacre. Someone above him. I never met her.\"","Mara watched his face. There was no sudden restoration of memory, no convenient certainty. Only a man deciding whether to let the unknown govern his next act.","\"Then we need to preserve what you heard,\" he said. \"And distinguish it from what you know firsthand.\"","Nessa swallowed. \"You may not like what we find.\"","\"That isn't the standard.\"","Outside, a car slowed beside the window. Nessa flinched so hard that the towel fell. Cassian glanced towards the street while Noor moved closer to the counter.","The car passed.","Nobody pretended that fear had been unreasonable merely because it had not become danger that time.","Nessa showed them a transfer message sent that morning. Reed had left Kingfisher in a private ambulance. The destination field was blank. Beneath it stood a service code and a direction to route all enquiries through the foundation's external office.","\"I put him in the car yesterday,\" she said. \"He wanted his daughter's picture. I shouldn't have taken him without approval. Today they said the outing had unsettled him.\"","\"Did you observe that?\" Noor asked.","\"No. He looked frightened when they came for him. That is all I saw.\"","Mara felt anger rise, a hot lump she could neither swallow nor use. She wanted to promise that they would find him before morning. She wanted to give Nessa the certainty she had once given vulnerable residents.","Instead she asked for the service code.","Nessa copied K-17 on the back of a receipt. As Mara put it in her pocket, the laundry's front door opened.","Two men stood beneath the light. Neither carried washing.","One looked directly at Nessa.","\"There you are,\" he said."],"questions":[{"type":"inference","stem":"Why does Mara ask Nessa for the service code rather than promise to find Reed before morning?","options":["She chooses a usable lead instead of reassurance she cannot guarantee.","She now regards the blank destination as a sufficient explanation.","She believes Her hand left a small damp print on the counter.","\"I'm not on shift,\" she said.","The taller man smiled with the particular patience of someone who expected a disagreement to be brief. He wore an overcoat too fine for the rain outside. His companion remained by the door, watching the washing machines turn.","\"Mr. Dacre is concerned. You left without completing your report.\"","\"I completed the handover.\"","\"Not that report.\"","Cassian moved no closer. \"Do you employ her?\"","The man looked at him, and recognition altered his posture. \"I represent the service provider. This is a household matter.\"","\"It's an employment matter,\" Nessa said. Her voice shook, but the distinction held. \"I don't live at Kingfisher.\"","Mara had expected to intervene. Instead, she found herself learning how to stand beside someone without stepping in front of her. She kept a clear path between Nessa and the back door. Noor remained seated, hands visible on her knees.","\"Your contract contains a duty of discretion,\" the man said.","\"And does it contain a duty to return with you?\" Cassian asked.","\"We are offering transport.\"","\"Then she can refuse.\"","\"I refuse,\" Nessa said.","A machine completed its cycle with a cheerful series of beeps. The needlessness of that ordinary sound nearly made Mara laugh. An argument that might have changed a life had ended beside a sweater someone would soon have to fold.","The man placed a card on the counter. \"We will expect a proper explanation tomorrow.\"","\"Send your questions in writing,\" Noor said.","He looked towards the window, apparently deciding how the scene would appear to a passerby. At last, he left. His companion followed without speaking.","Nobody called it a victory.","\"They have my mother's address,\" Nessa whispered.","Mara's first impulse was to pledge protection at all costs. She remembered how many people had used that phrase before offering a bill for it.","\"Does she know where you work?\" she asked instead.","Nessa nodded.","\"Can you tell her what happened, without sending any patient information? Then we can consider what she needs tonight.\"","The call lasted six minutes. Nessa cried once, quietly, then became irritated when her mother asked whether she had eaten. By the end she sounded more like an individual and less like a witness waiting to be stored somewhere safe.","Holt agreed to meet them at a solicitor's office. Nessa could receive independent advice about the records, her employment, and the risk to residents. Her cooperation would not entitle the others to direct her next move. Cassian said this explicitly, and Mara saw the tension ease a little from Nessa's face.","They left by different doors. June joined Nessa for the journey; Noor accompanied her to the office. Mara and Cassian walked towards the tram stop with a copy of the transport code and no new certainty about Reed's location.","Rain glittered on the pavement. A shop window displayed a bride and groom surrounded by timeless claims about devotion. The plastic bride wore a necklace larger than Mara's monthly rent.","\"Do you think they're happy?\" she asked.","Cassian considered the display. \"The groom appears to have no hands. I imagine that limits interpersonal conflict.\"","She laughed. It startled her after the drawn-out strain of the evening.","\"You were good in there,\" she said.","\"So was Nessa.\"","\"You allowed her to be.\"","He glanced at her. \"It wasn't mine to allow.\"","That answer would have sounded polished from another man. From him, after the minute-by-minute care of the laundry encounter, it seemed substantially more difficult and more useful.","Her borrowed blouse caught a gust of wind beneath the coat. Mara ate on the counter while Cassian tried to find a clean spoon. The dog, now named by popular staff agreement as Admiral, watched him with the optimism of a budding thief.","\"Your aunt will deride this household for weeks,\" Mara said.","\"She has been doing so for years. We shouldn't inflate our contribution.\"","\"Does she resent me?\"","\"She resents unpredictability. You are a high achiever in that field.\"","Mara gave him an admiring little bow. \"My sole scholarship.\"","\"I thought you refused one.\"","\"I refuse a broad range of things. It helps me maintain equality in a world full of offers.\"","He smiled, then his expression became more considered.","\"The workshop order,\" he said. \"An independent advocate has reviewed it. The allegation concerns an object a former client claims you failed to return.\"","\"The walnut cabinet?\"","\"Yes.\"","\"He hasn't paid me. The agreement permits me to hold it until he does.\"","\"Do you have that agreement?\"","\"In the workshop.\"","For a moment, neither spoke. Then Mara began to laugh without amusement.","\"An extravagant accumulation of inconvenience,\" she said.","\"Your landlady may have a copy of the invoice. The client paid a deposit into her business account when yours was frozen last winter.\"","She stared at him. \"How do you know that?\"","\"She told the advocate. Then she demanded a fax number, because she distrusts any legal institution that cannot cope with a fax.\"","Mara sat down. The relief hurt. It was one thing to imagine returning for her tools; another to discover that someone had kept a record when she herself had barely managed to keep going.","\"I ought to have told you earlier,\" Cassian said.","\"No. You ought to let me feel grateful without finding an error in yourself to explain it.\"","He accepted the correction with visible effort. She liked him more for the effort than she would have liked effortless grace.","After dinner they prepared the evidence copies. Ivo brought a spare recorder, and June called with an update from Nessa's solicitor. They created a sequence record of when each item had been found and who had handled it. Every gap remained a gap. Nobody revised it to make the account look better.","Nessa's first batch of messages arrived through her solicitor. Some concerned ordinary appointments; others instructed staff to describe the same cancelled visit differently to the resident and the family. June wanted to show how the phrases changed. Noor asked her to preserve the distinction between those instructions and evidence that anyone had actually followed them.","\"We have Nessa's account of some occasions,\" June said.","\"Then say some occasions.\"","June drew a line through her first sentence. Mara watched without interrupting. There was no humiliation in the revision. The small surrender of a stronger claim made the remaining one harder to dismiss.","A later message recorded a resident asking to leave. No answer followed in the copied thread. Mara felt herself reach towards the worst explanation, then stopped. A missing reply was a reason to find the rest of the exchange. It was not the rest of the exchange.","Cassian glanced at her notes and nodded. He did not praise her as if she had passed his examination. He simply treated the correction as work they owed the person whose voice was missing.","The magnetic strip stayed with Cassian. Mara retained the invoice and the flower. A sealed auxiliary copy would be stored independently, but private patient details would not be placed on a public site merely to make their enemies afraid.","\"Transparency doesn't mean undressing the vulnerable,\" Noor said through the phone.","Mara thought of Dacre's claims about confidential care. A good principle could be made into a closet; its name did not tell you whether someone had been locked inside.","They worked until the silver flower reflected the pale beginning of morning. Ivo examined the hinge again and found a later modification around the recording compartment. It had been fitted to an older mechanism. Elena's workmanship was present, but that did not mean she had created every part.","\"I was too quick at dinner,\" Mara said.","\"You identified her work,\" Cassian replied. \"That part stands. We can correct the scope of the claim.\"","She touched the small petal her mother had shaped. \"People will say I changed the story.\"","\"Then we give them the original and the correction.\"","It was harder than defending a claim at all costs. It was also the only answer Mara could bear to keep.","At sunrise the others left. She remained at the kitchen table, too tired to decide whether to sleep.
